Document Control
Volume 4 is the controlled engineering realization of The Eye. It converts frozen 
constitutional and architectural authority into testable construction, runtime, security, 
reliability, delivery, and acceptance obligations.
CONTROL VALUE
Product The Eye
Tagline The Operating System for Strategic Intelligence
Controlled document Volume 4 — Engineering Specification
Edition 1.0
Issue date 1 August 2026
Status Controlled Engineering Baseline
Binding sources Volume 0 — Product Constitution v1.0; Volume 3 — Technical Architecture v1.0
Primary audience CTO, principal and staff engineers, architecture, AI and data engineering, security, privacy, 
SRE, release engineering, quality, governance, product and programme leadership
Normative scope Deployable behavior, state ownership, APIs and events, object semantics, workflows, agents, 
models, trust, resilience, operations, delivery, tests, conformance, and acceptance
Downstream inheritance Volume 5 — AI Architecture; Volume 6 — Infrastructure Architecture; Volume 7 — Data 
Platform; implementation repositories and runbooks
Normative language
SHALL, MUST, SHALL NOT, and MUST NOT are binding. MAY permits an implementation choice inside 
a frozen semantic boundary. Requirements carry stable ES-CC-NNN identities. Conformance requires 
verifiable evidence for the exact artifact, configuration, environment, deployment profile, and scope under 
review.
CONTROLLING RULE
Volume 0 controls constitutional meaning. Volume 3 controls architecture. Volume 4 controls 
engineering realization. Lower-level specifications add precision but cannot weaken, rename, 
bypass, or silently reinterpret higher authority.

Engineering Charter
The engineering baseline is complete only when every material behavior has one 
owner, one authoritative state boundary, a versioned contract, explicit failure semantics, 
measurable quality, deployment-parity evidence, and a governed release path.
ENGINEERING 
COMMITMENT REQUIRED CONSTRUCTION CONSEQUENCE
One strategic operating system Services, agents, models, interfaces, and surfaces implement one connected product contract; 
they do not become independent intelligence products.
Ten canonical layers Every deployable unit maps to stable layer and component identities while preserving distinct 
authority and failure boundaries.
Knowledge Graph centrality Shared semantic identity and relationships are resolved through governed graph contracts, 
never ad hoc identifiers or private relationship stores.
Temporal and provenance 
completeness
Canonical products preserve time, truth state, source evidence, transformation, model, agent, 
human, policy, correction, and supersession lineage.
Human final authority Consequential institutional decisions and irreversible effects stop at an authenticated human 
gate.
Bounded intelligence execution Agents, models, tools, and workflows execute under explicit purpose, capability, quality, 
budget, supervision, and stop conditions.
Safe degradation Failure may reduce capability but never fabricates success, currency, confidence, 
completeness, authorization, provenance, or approval.
Immutable delivery One signed candidate advances across environments and deployments with reproducible 
evidence, staged rollout, monitoring, and rollback.
Deployment semantic parity SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise pass the same canonical conformance fixtures and 
preserve customer portability.
Definition of engineering complete
• Controlling requirements, interfaces, schemas, policies, models, agents, workflows, configuration, 
and migrations are versioned and owned.
• Normal, partial, abstained, stale, denied, failed, recovering, and terminal behavior is explicit and 
testable.
• Security, privacy, sovereignty, audit, quality, resilience, recovery, and observability evidence closes 
for the intended consequence and deployment scope.
• Operations can diagnose, constrain, recover, explain, challenge, export, and retire the capability 
without uncontrolled access or hidden state.
• A signed operational acceptance record identifies exact scope, independent authorities, open 
conditions, review, and withdrawal criteria.
CONSTITUTIONAL INHERITANCE  /  C-004  ·  C-005  ·  C-008  ·  C-035  ·  C-042  ·  C-049  ·  C-052

Executive Engineering Statement
Volume 4 defines the engineering system that makes The Eye persistent, explainable, 
recoverable, governable, and safe to operate at enterprise and sovereign scale.
The central engineering problem is not model invocation. It is preserving institutional meaning and 
authority while a continuous system observes heterogeneous sources, constructs connected knowledge, 
maintains digital representations, forecasts and simulates uncertain futures, prepares decision packages, 
records human decisions, governs commitments, measures outcomes, and learns without rewriting 
history.
Figure 1. The Closed Engineering Loop. Each phase commits governed state and evidence for the next.
SYSTEM PLANE ENGINEERING OBLIGATION
Canonical product plane Typed intelligence objects preserve identity, version, temporal truth, truth state, provenance, 
policy, quality, correction, and lifecycle.
Execution plane Services, events, workflows, human gates, agents, models, and tools coordinate through durable 
and versioned contracts.
State plane One accountable component owns each canonical lifecycle; projections remain rebuildable and 
cross-owner writes are prohibited.
Trust plane Identity, authorization, classification, rights, privacy, sovereignty, cryptography, audit, supply 
chain, and isolation are enforced at material boundaries.
Reliability plane Quality, SLOs, capacity, failure domains, degradation, recovery, observability, incidents, and 
lifecycle operations describe customer-visible product state.
Delivery plane Repositories, configuration, tests, immutable promotion, packaging, acceptance, and governance 
preserve conformance from source to operation.
ENGINEERING THESIS
The Eye may evolve in technology, deployment, and implementation. It remains one product only 
when canonical meaning, human authority, evidence, control, temporal reconstruction, and 
portability are invariant and continuously proven.
CONSTITUTIONAL INHERITANCE  /  C-001  ·  C-004  ·  C-005  ·  C-008  ·  C-035  ·  C-042  ·  C-052

1  Engineering Authority and Scope
Volume 4 converts the controlled architecture into a testable engineering system. It 
specifies behavior, ownership, contracts, state transitions, failure handling, and 
evidence required before any implementation may represent itself as The Eye.
Figure 2. Engineering Authority Chain. Lower specifications add precision without weakening controlling meaning.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Authority Volume 0 controls constitutional invariants; Volume 3 controls architecture; Volume 4 controls 
engineering realization.
Applies to All first-party services, agents, models, data products, user surfaces, extensions, marketplace 
packages, deployment modes, and operational tooling.
Does not authorize A new product category, renamed canonical layer, alternate truth system, autonomous final decision 
right, or deployment-specific semantic fork.
Conformance result Conformant, conditionally conformant under a recorded exception, or non-conformant. Silence is 
never approval.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-01-001
Every deployable unit SHALL identify its owning canonical layer, 
component ID, state boundary, contracts, quality obligations, and 
accountable engineering owner.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-01-002
Every material behavior SHALL trace to an approved 
requirement in this volume and to the controlling architecture 
chapter or invariant.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-01-003
Implementation convenience SHALL NOT collapse canonical 
layers, bypass human authority, erase provenance, or create a 
second source of strategic truth.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-01-004
A product surface MAY aggregate capabilities, but the underlying 
ownership, authorization, evidence, and failure states SHALL 
remain separable and testable.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-01-005
A release SHALL be rejected when conformance evidence is 
absent, stale, unverifiable, or scoped to a different artifact 
version.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Unmapped component or service Block production registration until ownership and inheritance are recorded.
Architecture conflict Stop implementation promotion and open a governed exception or amendment workflow.

FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Undeclared customer-visible 
behavior
Treat as non-conformant product behavior and remove or place behind a disabled 
controlled flag.
Acceptance evidence
• Component inventory proves complete mapping to the ten canonical layers.
• Release manifest identifies requirement, interface, object, policy, and test versions.
• Architecture conformance review records no unresolved category or authority conflict.
• Production registry rejects unsigned or unmapped workloads and packages.

2  Specification Hierarchy and Conformance
Engineering decisions inherit downward through an explicit control chain. Lower-level 
specifications may add precision, but they may not silently weaken, reinterpret, or 
supersede higher authority.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Control order Product Constitution -> Technical Architecture -> Engineering Specification -> domain specifications 
-> implementation -> verification evidence.
Normative artifact A versioned document, schema, policy, test suite, manifest, or decision record that is approved by 
its accountable authority.
Conflict rule The highest controlling authority prevails until an explicit amendment is ratified and propagated.
Exception rule Exceptions are narrow, owned, time-bounded, monitored, reversible, and prohibited from changing 
constitutional meaning.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-02-001 Every normative artifact SHALL declare authority, owner, status, 
version, effective time, supersession, and dependent artifacts.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-02-002 Every implementation repository SHALL expose a machine-
readable conformance manifest at build and release time.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-02-003 A conditional exception SHALL carry scope, rationale, risk 
acceptance, compensating controls, expiry, and closure criteria. Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-02-004 No component owner MAY approve an exception that expands 
that component's authority or removes an independent control.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-02-005
Conformance SHALL be re-evaluated when controlling 
specifications, schemas, policies, models, deployment 
constraints, or dependencies change.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Conflicting specifications Resolve against the hierarchy; quarantine the lower artifact until corrected.
Expired exception Fail the affected release or workload closed unless an authorized renewal is recorded 
before expiry.
Missing evidence Return an indeterminate conformance result and deny production promotion.

3  Normative Requirement Model
Requirements are stable engineering objects, not prose annotations. Each requirement 
has a durable identity, an owning authority, explicit verification, and a lifecycle that 
survives document and implementation revisions.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Identifier ES-CC-NNN, where CC is the two-digit chapter and NNN is the ordered requirement within that 
chapter.
Modality SHALL and MUST are mandatory; SHALL NOT and MUST NOT are prohibitions; MAY is an 
allowed choice inside the stated boundary.
Verification Inspection, analysis, automated test, fault injection, security test, operational exercise, or 
accountable review.
Lifecycle Proposed -> reviewed -> accepted -> implemented -> verified -> released -> monitored -> 
superseded or retired.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-03-001
A mandatory requirement SHALL have at least one executable or 
independently reviewable verification method before 
implementation is accepted.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-03-002
Requirement text SHALL state one controlling obligation and 
avoid hidden compound conditions that cannot be tested 
independently.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-03-003 Derived requirements SHALL reference their parent requirement 
and SHALL NOT weaken its acceptance threshold.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-03-004
A superseded requirement SHALL remain resolvable for Decision 
Replay, incident analysis, audit, and historical conformance 
reconstruction.
Operational exercise
Time-stamped exercise record with 
telemetry, operators, impact, 
recovery, and closure evidence.
ES-03-005
Metrics used as acceptance evidence SHALL declare population, 
window, unit, aggregation, exclusions, threshold, and data 
lineage.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Ambiguous requirement Return it to the owning authority before implementation or test design begins.
Untestable requirement Classify as incomplete and block acceptance until a verification method exists.
Conflicting acceptance evidence Preserve both results, open a challenge, and withhold conformance.

• Requirement registry contains unique IDs and no unresolved duplicate authority.
• Every mandatory requirement maps to one or more verification records.
• Historical versions remain queryable with valid and system time.
• Release evidence shows the exact requirement set evaluated for the artifact.

4  Product Boundary and Engineering Decomposition
The Eye is one Strategic Intelligence Operating System. Engineering decomposition 
creates independently owned and deployable units without creating feature islands, 
parallel truth, or deployment-specific products.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Canonical layer The stable responsibility and state boundary defined by Volume 3.
Logical component A stable architecture capability identified by Lx-Cyy.
Deployable unit One or more processes released, scaled, secured, and observed as a unit while preserving logical 
ownership.
Projection A rebuildable access representation; never an undeclared replacement for canonical state.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-04-001 Each logical component SHALL have exactly one accountable 
owning team even when several deployable units realize it.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-04-002
A deployable unit MAY implement multiple components only 
when state ownership, contracts, policy decisions, telemetry, and 
failure isolation remain distinguishable.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-04-003
Direct database coupling across component ownership 
boundaries SHALL be prohibited; integration SHALL use a 
declared contract or governed projection.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-04-004
User interfaces, chat, dashboards, reports, and APIs SHALL 
remain access modes over the operating system and SHALL 
NOT become independent product truth stores.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-04-005
Shared libraries SHALL contain non-authoritative reusable 
mechanics; they SHALL NOT conceal cross-layer business state 
or decision authority.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Circular state ownership Refactor ownership before production; no shared-write compromise is permitted.
Undeclared shared database Stop writes, identify the authoritative owner, and migrate consumers to contracts or 
projections.
Access surface creates state Route the state transition through its owning component and preserve the surface as caller 
context.

• Dependency graph contains no cross-boundary database write path.

• Projection rebuild tests prove canonical recovery without the projection store.
• Access-mode tests show identical authority and truth behavior across UI, API, and agent calls.

5  Engineering Principles
The engineering system preserves the closed strategic loop by making truth, time, 
provenance, authority, uncertainty, failure, and learning explicit in every relevant 
component and transition.
Figure 3. The Closed Engineering Loop. Each phase commits governed state and evidence for the next.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Truthful state Observed, claimed, inferred, assessed, decided, synthetic, withdrawn, and superseded information 
remain distinguishable.
Human authority AI prepares, reasons, forecasts, simulates, recommends, and explains; a named human role makes 
the final consequential decision.
Permanent memory Versioned institutional state remains reconstructable under lawful retention and deletion controls.
Safe change Learning proposes change; evaluation, approval, release, monitoring, rollback, and tenant isolation 
govern adoption.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-05-001
Every material output SHALL expose its evidence boundary, truth 
state, time scope, uncertainty, policy state, producing versions, 
and accountable owner.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-05-002
No automated path SHALL translate a recommendation into a 
final consequential decision without an authenticated human 
approval contract.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-05-003 Correction SHALL create new history and impact propagation; it 
SHALL NOT overwrite the record used by an earlier decision.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-05-004
Degraded capability SHALL be visible at the point of use and 
SHALL NOT present stale, partial, or substituted output as 
healthy.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-05-005
Learning SHALL produce governed proposals and SHALL NOT 
mutate production models, prompts, agents, policies, ontologies, 
or decision logic silently.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Unknown truth state Quarantine the object from decision-active use until classified or explicitly abstained.
Human approval unavailable Pause, expire, or escalate the workflow; never infer approval from timeout.

FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Lineage discontinuity Mark dependent products unfit and trigger impact analysis and repair.
Acceptance evidence
• End-to-end fixtures reconstruct source-to-outcome lineage.
• Decision tests prove an authenticated human gate for every final consequential transition.
• Correction replay reproduces prior state without hindsight contamination.
• Learning release tests prove evaluation, approval, monitoring, and rollback controls.

6  Cross-Volume Traceability
Traceability is an operational dependency graph linking constitutional authority, 
architecture, engineering requirements, code, schemas, policies, tests, releases, 
runtime evidence, decisions, and outcomes.
Figure 4. Traceability Mesh. Requirements remain connected from authority through production evidence.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Upstream Controlling invariants, architecture chapters, component and interface IDs, object definitions, and 
ADRs.
Engineering Requirement IDs, design records, code modules, schemas, policies, configurations, test suites, and 
owners.
Release Immutable artifact digests, dependency inventory, approval, conformance results, deployment 
constraints, and rollback target.
Runtime Workload, model, agent, policy, configuration, object, workflow, decision, and telemetry versions.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-06-001 Trace links SHALL be machine-readable, versioned, and 
queryable in both upstream and downstream directions.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-06-002 Every production artifact SHALL resolve to the source revision 
and requirement set from which it was built.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-06-003
Every material runtime output SHALL resolve to the exact 
service, schema, model, agent, policy, and configuration versions 
that produced it.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-06-004
A controlling change SHALL trigger deterministic impact analysis

7  Runtime Topology
The runtime topology separates customer intelligence domains, scalable intelligence 
cells, shared control services, edge collection zones, controlled execution gateways, 
and recovery domains without changing canonical semantics.
Figure 5. Runtime Topology. Customer work is isolated in Intelligence Cells and governed by shared control services.

default.

effects.

11  Component Ownership and Service Boundaries
Every logical component has one accountable owner, one declared state contract, one 
controlled interface surface, and explicit dependencies. Service count is an 
implementation variable; ownership is not.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Owner Accountable team responsible for behavior, interfaces, state, quality, security, operations, and 
conformance.
Boundary Declared inputs, outputs, owned state, dependencies, prohibited access, and failure behavior.
Service Deployable realization with immutable artifact identity and runtime ownership metadata.
Library Reusable stateless or local mechanics that cannot become an undeclared state or policy authority.

12  State Ownership and Persistence
Canonical state is owned by layer components and preserved as versioned records. 
Databases, graph engines, object stores, indexes, caches, and vector representations 
are implementation mechanisms with explicit authority classes.
Figure 6. State Authority. Every state class has one writer and an explicit reconstruction contract.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Canonical store Authoritative system of record for an owned object or transition.
Durable journal Append-only transition or event history sufficient for recovery, audit, and replay.
Projection Rebuildable query representation derived from canonical state.
Ephemeral state Cache, scratch, session, or execution state whose loss has declared recovery semantics.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-12-001
Every persisted dataset SHALL declare owner, authority class, 
schema, temporal model, retention, encryption, backup, 
recovery, and deletion behavior.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-12-002
Canonical records SHALL be immutable by version; updates 
SHALL create a new version or an owned state transition with 
prior history preserved.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-12-003 Projection state SHALL store source revision and build version 
and SHALL never be accepted as canonical during recovery. Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-12-004
Cross-store operations SHALL use a durable coordination 
pattern with causation, idempotency, reconciliation, and 
observable partial state.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-12-005 Backup and restore SHALL preserve domain isolation, key 
policy, temporal history, audit integrity, and deletion obligations. Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Canonical/projection divergence Prefer canonical state, mark the projection stale, rebuild, and report affected consumers.
Partial cross-store commit Resume or compensate from the durable transition record; never infer completion from one 
store.
Unknown dataset authority Prohibit decision-active use until ownership and authority are classified.

13  Cross-Cutting Control Enforcement
Trust, security, privacy, identity, policy, workflow, evaluation, audit, sovereignty, and 
operations are in-band engineering behavior applied at every material boundary.
Figure 7. Control Enforcement. Controls operate at every material read, transformation, action, and export boundary.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Policy enforcement point Interception boundary that evaluates a declared action against current authoritative policy.
Policy decision point Versioned authority that returns allow, deny, obligations, review, or indeterminate.
Evidence point Boundary that records material access, transformation, model or agent action, approval, decision, 
release, correction, or export.
Quality gate Boundary that prevents unfit products, models, agents, packages, or data from entering a higher-
trust state.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-13-001

and privileged administration.

for the exact provider, consumer,

Security test
Signed policy/security suite result

Time-stamped exercise record with 
telemetry, operators, impact, 
recovery, and closure evidence.
Failure semantics

Policy service unavailable Use an approved unexpired decision cache only where authorized; otherwise deny and 
expose control unavailability.
Obligation failure Abort or compensate the protected action and preserve partial evidence.
Bypass detected Terminate the path, contain the actor or workload, and open a security and conformance 
incident.

14  Deployment Semantic Parity
SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise are operating envelopes for one product. 
Physical topology and operational responsibility may differ; object meaning, authority, 
contracts, workflow, evidence, and conformance do not.
Figure 8. Deployment Semantic Parity. Physical responsibility may move; product meaning does not.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Invariant core Ten layers, canonical objects, truth and temporal model, provenance, human authority, workflow, 
audit, and external contracts.
Variable envelope Capacity, managed services, connectivity, locality, key custody, administration, model availability, 
support, and update cadence.
Capability constraint Declared unavailable or reduced function caused by an operating-envelope limit; never a hidden 
semantic substitute.
Portability proof The same conformance fixtures and canonical artifacts execute across all supported modes.

15  Canonical Service Contract
Every service exposes a uniform engineering contract that makes identity, purpose, 
ownership, data access, time, semantics, quality, failure, and operations explicit before 
transport-specific details.
Figure 9. Canonical Service Contract. Every boundary makes intent, authority, behavior, and evidence explicit.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Identity Service, component, layer, owner, artifact, workload, environment, domain, and caller assurance.
Behavior Operations, inputs, outputs, side effects, state transitions, invariants, and prohibited actions.
Quality Latency, availability, freshness, throughput, accuracy or fitness, capacity, and degradation contract.
Operations Dependencies, timeouts, retries, idempotency, health, telemetry, runbook, recovery, and support.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-15-001 A service SHALL register its contract before accepting production 
traffic or events.

16  Synchronous API Profile
Synchronous APIs serve bounded commands and queries that require an immediate 
admission or result contract. They preserve the canonical envelope and never hide 
durable asynchronous work behind an ambiguous success response.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
External profile Authenticated HTTPS resource and action APIs with explicit media type, schema version, deadline, 
idempotency, pagination, and error body.
Internal profile Authenticated typed RPC or equivalent request-response transport using the same semantic 
envelope.
Long-running work Returns an accepted operation reference whose durable state is queryable and event-producing.
Streaming Bounded server or bidirectional stream with admission, sequence, checkpoint, cancellation, and 
backpressure semantics.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-16-001
Every request SHALL carry correlation, causation when 
applicable, domain, principal or workload, purpose, deadline, 
schema, and idempotency context.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-16-002
Success status SHALL mean the documented state transition or 
query completed; admission to long-running work SHALL return a 
distinct accepted result.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-16-003
Clients SHALL be able to distinguish validation, authorization, 
policy, conflict, stale state, capacity, dependency, timeout, and 
internal failure.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-16-004 Pagination SHALL use opaque stable cursors bound to query, 
sort, policy, and consistency context. Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-16-005
APIs SHALL support cancellation or deadline propagation and 
SHALL prevent abandoned callers from creating uncontrolled 
work or side effects.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Deadline exceeded Stop safe work, preserve durable state, and return an unambiguous timeout or accepted-
operation reference.
Client retry Deduplicate through the operation's idempotency contract and return the prior result or 
current durable state.
Backpressure Reject or throttle with retry guidance and visible capacity state; do not queue without a 
declared bound.

17  Command Profile
Commands request owned behavior and may create durable state transitions. A 
command is directed to exactly one authority and returns an acceptance or rejection 
that is distinct from downstream completion.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Target Owning component and operation.
Preconditions Expected object version, workflow state, policy context, deadline, and required approval.
Identity Command ID, idempotency key, correlation, causation, domain, actor, workload, and delegation.
Result Rejected, completed synchronously, or accepted with durable operation and eventual event 
references.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-17-001 A command SHALL name one state-owning target and SHALL 
NOT broadcast an instruction to several competing authorities.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.

to domain, operation, caller, and declared validity window. Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-17-003 Precondition failures SHALL return current state references 
without applying a partial transition. Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-17-004 Accepted commands SHALL create a durable operation record 
before acknowledging acceptance.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-17-005
Command handlers SHALL emit committed domain events 
through the same transaction boundary or a recoverable outbox 
pattern.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Duplicate command Return the recorded result or operation state without repeating the material effect.
Concurrent version conflict Reject with current version and require explicit caller reconciliation.
Partial downstream work Resume or compensate from the durable operation state; do not report completion.

18  Query and Consistency Profile
Queries retrieve policy-filtered state without changing canonical ownership. Callers

their purpose.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Current Latest committed version visible under the selected consistency and policy context.
As-of State reconstructable at declared valid and system times without later contamination.
Revision-bound Result fixed to named object, graph, ontology, memory, model, policy, or projection revisions.
Coverage Declared completeness, freshness, source health, and known blind spots for the result set.

19  Event Profile
Domain events announce committed authoritative transitions. They are immutable facts 
about the platform state, not mutable messages, commands disguised as broadcasts, 
or unversioned integration payloads.
Figure 10. Command-To-Event Commit. State, event intent, and audit causation form one recoverable boundary.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Identity Globally unique event ID, event type, schema version, producer component, producer artifact, and 
domain.
Causality Correlation, causation, command, workflow, object, and trace references.
Time Occurrence, commit, publication, observation where applicable, and expiry.
Delivery Partition key, sequence, deduplication, retention, replay, and consumer checkpoint contract.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-19-001 An event SHALL be published only after the announced state 
transition is durable in the owning component.
Automated 
conformance test

for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.

become uncontrolled data replicas.
Negative 
conformance test

prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-19-003 Consumers SHALL be idempotent and SHALL persist 
checkpoints only after their owned effects are durable.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-19-004
Ordering SHALL be guaranteed only within a declared partition 
and sequence scope; consumers SHALL handle cross-partition

Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer,

ES-19-005

Negative 
conformance test

prohibited path is denied and

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Duplicate delivery Deduplicate by event identity and consumer effect key.

invalid order.
Poison event Quarantine with full evidence, continue unaffected partitions where safe, and surface 
consumer lag and impact.

21  Error, Abstention, and Partial-Result Model
Errors are typed engineering products with safe disclosure, causation, retry guidance, 
user impact, and audit evidence. Analytical abstention and partial results are first-class 
outcomes, not generic failures.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Validation Input, schema, semantic, precondition, or compatibility failure before owned behavior.
Control Authentication, authorization, policy, approval, classification, sovereignty, or safety decision.
Operational Capacity, dependency, timeout, conflict, unavailable, degraded, or internal failure.
Intelligence Insufficient evidence, low fitness, uncertainty breach, incoherent scenario, invalid simulation, or 
required human judgment.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-21-001
Every error SHALL include a stable code, category, safe 
message, correlation reference, retry class, owning component, 
and support or remediation path.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-21-002
Internal details, secrets, unauthorized object existence, stack 
traces, and sensitive policy logic SHALL not be exposed to 
untrusted callers.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-21-003
Abstention SHALL identify the unmet evidence, quality, 
uncertainty, policy, or authority condition and the path to review 
or additional information.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-21-004
Partial results SHALL declare missing scope, unavailable 
dependencies, freshness, confidence, affected conclusions, and 
whether decision-active use is prohibited.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-21-005
Retry guidance SHALL distinguish never, after caller correction, 
after policy or approval, after a stated delay, and safe idempotent 
retry.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Unknown error Map to a non-disclosing internal error, preserve detailed evidence privately, and open 
defect analysis.
Abstention converted to success Fail conformance and invalidate downstream products that relied on the false success.
Partial result used as complete Mark affected decisions and briefings, propagate impact, and require review.
Acceptance evidence
• Error catalog tests cover every public and internal code and declared retry class.
• Security tests verify non-disclosure and correlation-based supportability.
• Analytical fixtures force abstention and partial-result paths.
• Downstream tests prove partial and abstained state cannot be silently promoted.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE

22  Idempotency, Ordering, Retry, and Compensation
Distributed execution assumes duplication, reordering, delay, partial failure, and replay. 
Correctness comes from explicit semantics and durable state, not from optimistic 
exactly-once claims.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Idempotency scope Domain, operation, actor or workload, target, key, and validity window.
Ordering scope Named aggregate or partition with monotonic sequence and gap behavior.
Retry policy Attempt limit, backoff, jitter, deadline, retryable codes, budget, and ownership.
Compensation Owned reversal or reconciliation action for an already committed external or cross-boundary effect.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-22-001 Every material side effect SHALL have a stable deduplication or 
reconciliation strategy before release.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-22-002
Retry loops SHALL be bounded by deadline and budget and 
SHALL not amplify load, bypass policy, or duplicate external 
effects.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-22-003
Ordering assumptions SHALL be local and declared; global 
ordering SHALL not be inferred from timestamps or transport 
position.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-22-004 Compensation SHALL be a new evidenced transition and SHALL 
not delete the original action or pretend it never occurred. Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-22-005 Workflow and event replay SHALL preserve original causation 
while recording replay identity, initiator, reason, and result. Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Idempotency record unavailable Reject or pause material effects unless an approved alternate reconciliation path exists.
Sequence gap Mark state incomplete and trigger bounded wait, fetch, or reconciliation.
Compensation failure Escalate with original and compensation evidence; do not declare rollback complete.
Acceptance evidence
• Chaos fixtures inject duplicates, reorder, delay, retry storms, crash-after-effect, and crash-before-
record.
• External-effect tests prove one material effect per idempotency scope.
• Sequence recovery tests reconcile gaps and late arrivals.
• Compensation tests preserve original evidence and expose residual state.

23  Versioning, Compatibility, and Contract Registry
Schemas, APIs, events, services, policies, models, agents, packages, and configuration 
profiles evolve through explicit compatibility classes and a governed registry.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Additive New optional meaning that existing conformant consumers safely ignore.
Behavioral Same shape with changed semantics, thresholds, defaults, policy, or side effects; treated as 
compatibility-significant.
Breaking Removes, renames, retypes, reinterprets, or changes required ordering, authority, truth, time, or 
error semantics.
Retirement Time-bound removal after inventory, notice, migration, verification, and residual-consumer closure.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-23-001
Every contract SHALL have a stable identity, semantic version, 
owner, status, compatibility class, effective time, and machine-
readable schema or definition.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-23-002 Behavioral changes SHALL be versioned even when wire shape 
remains unchanged.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-23-003
Breaking changes SHALL use parallel compatibility, explicit 
migration, consumer verification, and governed retirement; in-
place reinterpretation is prohibited.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-23-004 Consumers SHALL declare supported version ranges and 
SHALL fail visibly when no compatible version exists.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-23-005
The registry SHALL prevent publication of duplicate authority, 
incompatible reuse of identity, invalid supersession, or unsigned 
production contracts.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
No compatible contract Reject admission or interaction and expose the resolved compatibility conflict.
Retired consumer remains Hold retirement or isolate the non-conformant consumer under an authorized time-bound 
exception.
Registry unavailable Use only a verified unexpired local snapshot and prohibit unregistered change.
Acceptance evidence
• Compatibility suite checks wire, semantic, policy, error, ordering, and side-effect behavior.
• Consumer inventory reconciles declared and observed version use.
• Migration tests cover dual-read or dual-publish, cutover, rollback, and historical replay.
• Registry integrity tests reject identity reuse, invalid signatures, and supersession cycles.

24  Canonical Intelligence Object Header
Every material intelligence product inherits one universal header so identity, version, 
time, truth, evidence, control, quality, and operational lineage survive all service, agent, 
model, human, and deployment boundaries.
Figure 12. Canonical Intelligence Object. The universal header makes every product governable and replayable.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION

correlation, causation, and audit references.

containing signed context supplies identical immutable values 
and reconstruction is lossless.
Automated 
conformance test

for the exact provider, consumer,

25  Identity, Naming, and Tenancy
Stable identity separates what an object, actor, workload, model, agent, contract, or

Engineering contract

Opaque ID Globally unique, non-semantic, immutable identifier used for durable references.

Display name Localized and mutable label never used as authorization or referential identity.
Domain scope Every customer-controlled identity resolves inside one Customer Intelligence Domain unless a 
governed global authority explicitly owns it.

26  Temporal Truth Engineering
The platform distinguishes when something occurred, when it was observed, when an 
assertion applies, and when The Eye recorded each version. This temporal model is 
mandatory for reconstruction and Decision Replay.
Figure 13. Temporal Truth Record. Four time dimensions preserve the world, observation, applicability, and knowledge history.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Event time When the represented occurrence happened in the world.
Observation time When a source or The Eye observed or received it.
Valid time Interval in the world for which the assertion or state applies.
System time Interval during which a specific version was recorded as current in The Eye.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-26-001

time dimensions with timezone, precision, source, and 
uncertainty.

conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-26-002

component and SHALL be monotonic within the object's version 
history.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-26-003
As-of queries SHALL accept explicit valid and system time and 
SHALL not contaminate results with later corrections or 
knowledge.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-26-004
Unknown, approximate, bounded, recurring, and conflicting time 
SHALL be represented explicitly rather than forced into false 
precision.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-26-005 Clock quality, skew, and synchronization state SHALL be

Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Invalid temporal interval Reject canonical commit and retain the proposal for correction.
Clock uncertainty exceeds contract Use sequence or authoritative commit order and expose temporal uncertainty.
Historical reconstruction gap Return explicit missing range and affected objects; never substitute current state.

27  Truth State, Confidence, and Uncertainty
Truth state identifies what kind of knowledge an object represents; confidence and 
uncertainty characterize support and limits. Neither is a substitute for evidence or 
human authority.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Observed Directly preserved from an identified source or instrument.
Claimed / inferred / 
assessed Attributed assertion, derived result, or accountable analytical judgment.
Decided Proposition or option incorporated into a recorded human decision within its scope.
Synthetic / withdrawn / 
superseded Scenario or simulation state, or information no longer decision-active because history changed.

incident.

28  Provenance and Lineage Engineering
Provenance forms a traversable graph from source evidence through transformations, 
graph and memory revisions, twins, models, agents, forecasts, scenarios, simulations, 
recommendations, human decisions, commitments, and outcomes.
Figure 14. Provenance And Impact Graph. Backward explanation and forward impact are two views of the same lineage.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Source lineage Authority, rights, source identity, acquisition, custody, checksum, and preserved evidence.

commitment.
Forward impact Decision-active products and workflows that depend on a changed, corrected, withdrawn, or unfit 
object.

30  Correction, Supersession, and Projection Rebuild
Corrections change what is decision-active without erasing what was previously 
recorded or knowable. Supersession and withdrawal propagate through durable impact 
analysis, projection invalidation, review, and closure.
Figure 15. Correction Propagation. Corrections change decision-active state and initiate durable downstream review.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Correction New version or linked object that replaces, adjudicates, merges, splits, or qualifies prior information.
Withdrawal Authority-backed removal from decision-active use while preserving history and reason.
Supersession Ordered relationship identifying the version now applicable under stated time and policy scope.
Projection rebuild Deterministic regeneration of derived query state from corrected canonical history.
Normative requirements

31  World Observation Layer
Maintain a measurable, governed field of view across authorized external, internal,

CONTRACT ENGINEERING DEFINITION
Layer authority Maintain a measurable, governed field of view across authorized external, internal, physical, and 
networked sources.
Boundary Owns source contracts, acquisition, raw preservation, source health, coverage, and correction 
intake. It does not infer institutional truth or publish recommendations.
Authoritative state Source contracts and credential references; Connector configuration and checkpoints; Immutable 
raw evidence; Acquisition and source-health history; Coverage map and blind spots.
Primary inputs Authorized source definitions; Credentials and collection authority; Schedules, subscriptions, and 
stream endpoints; Customer purpose and coverage requirements.
Canonical outputs Observation Records; Evidence Objects; Source health and freshness state; Coverage gaps; 
Correction and withdrawal events.
Quality dimensions coverage, freshness, latency, completeness, authenticity, collection success, correction propagation 
time.

33  Enterprise Memory
Preserve durable, permission-aware organizational and world memory across evidence,

Figure 18. State Authority. Every state class has one writer and an explicit reconstruction contract.
Layer implementation contract

Layer authority Preserve durable, permission-aware organizational and world memory across evidence, analyses, 
decisions, actions, outcomes, and lessons.
Boundary
Owns persistence, version history, retention, legal hold, correction, retrieval indexes, and memory 
access policy. It does not convert retrieval frequency or model output into accepted organizational 
truth.
Authoritative state Memory objects and versions; Retention and legal-hold state; Retrieval indexes; Correction and 
tombstone history; Archive, export, and deletion evidence.
Primary inputs Evidence and intelligence objects; Documents and communications; Graph, twin, decision, and 
outcome artifacts; Retention, deletion, and legal-hold policy.
Canonical outputs Versioned memory entries; Permission-aware retrieval context; Historical reconstruction; Retention 
and deletion evidence; Strategic record packages.
Quality dimensions durability, retrieval relevance, permission correctness, temporal completeness, reconstruction 
success, retention compliance, deletion verification.

Provenance links; Graph revision and stewardship history.

and dependency views; Strategy Graph context.

orphan rate, query latency, revision propagation.

• Synthetic relationships are explicitly marked
Degraded behavior and acceptance

operator action, recovery, and reconciliation evidence.

operator action, recovery, and reconciliation evidence.

Figure 20. Temporal Truth Record. Four time dimensions preserve the world, observation, applicability, and knowledge history.

products, markets, regulations, infrastructure, processes, and organization.
Boundary Owns twin definitions, evidence boundaries, state estimation, model bindings, snapshots, branches,

dependency completeness.

36  Prediction Engine
Estimate future variables, events, and system states across the canonical 30-day, 90-
day, 180-day, 1-year, 3-year, and 5-year horizons.
Figure 21. Governed Model Gateway. Task and policy constraints select a fit, approved, and available model.
Layer implementation contract
CONTRACT ENGINEERING DEFINITION
Layer authority Estimate future variables, events, and system states across the canonical 30-day, 90-day, 180-day, 
1-year, 3-year, and 5-year horizons.
Boundary
Owns forecast definitions, horizon methods, feature context, model routing, ensembles, calibration, 
uncertainty, sensitivity, evaluation, drift, and forecast versions. It does not claim certainty or select 
institutional action.
Authoritative state Forecast definitions; Feature and model bindings; Forecast versions; Calibration and backtest 
history; Drift, expiry, and withdrawal state.
Primary inputs Governed evidence and graph context; Twin snapshots; Forecast definitions and horizons; 
Approved models, features, and evaluation thresholds.
Canonical outputs Forecast Packages; Probability distributions and confidence; Drivers and sensitivity; Calibration and 
fitness state; Refresh triggers and failure conditions.
Quality dimensions calibration, proper scoring rules, coverage, bias, sharpness, stability, drift, latency, cost, decision 
relevance.

37  Scenario Intelligence
Maintain a coherent, monitored portfolio of multiple plausible futures under uncertainty 
without allowing one forecast to masquerade as destiny.
Figure 22. Proactive Warning Runtime. Weak signals become accountable attention only after materiality and ownership controls.
Layer implementation contract
CONTRACT ENGINEERING DEFINITION
Layer authority Maintain a coherent, monitored portfolio of multiple plausible futures under uncertainty without 
allowing one forecast to masquerade as destiny.
Boundary
Owns scenario definitions, assumptions, actors, drivers, branches, indicators, signposts, 
interventions, coherence, comparison, review cadence, and portfolio state. It does not execute 
simulations or approve decisions.
Authoritative state Scenario definitions and branches; Assumptions, actors, drivers, and mechanisms; Indicators and 
signposts; Coherence and confidence state; Review and retirement history.
Primary inputs Forecast Packages; Twin snapshots and graph context; Objectives, assumptions, risks, and 
opportunities; Human and agent hypotheses.
Canonical outputs Scenario branches and portfolios; Assumption registers; Indicators and signposts; Branch 
confidence and coherence state; Simulation and decision context.
Quality dimensions assumption coverage, internal coherence, branch diversity, indicator freshness, signpost 
discrimination, review timeliness, decision coverage.

38  Simulation Engine
Run reproducible experiments against declared twin versions, scenarios, assumptions, 
interventions, models, constraints, and seeds before institutional commitment.
Figure 23. Product Quality Contract. Operational health and intelligence fitness remain distinct but connected.
Layer implementation contract
CONTRACT ENGINEERING DEFINITION
Layer authority Run reproducible experiments against declared twin versions, scenarios, assumptions, 
interventions, models, constraints, and seeds before institutional commitment.
Boundary
Owns experiment definitions, run orchestration, version resolution, constraints, solver adapters, 
impact analysis, sensitivity, validation, and reproducibility evidence. It does not silently alter 
production state or approve action.
Authoritative state Experiment definitions; Resolved run manifests; Execution and resource state; Outputs and 
sensitivity; Validation, challenge, and invalidation history.
Primary inputs Versioned twin and scenario branches; Interventions and constraints; Approved models and solver 
adapters; Run budgets, policies, and validation criteria.
Canonical outputs Simulation Runs; Direct and second-order impacts; Sensitivity and uncertainty; Reproducibility 
package; Validation and challenge record.
Quality dimensions reproducibility, numerical stability, constraint satisfaction, sensitivity coverage, validation status, run 
latency, cost, failure containment.

39  Decision Intelligence
Assemble complete, explainable choice packages and compare viable alternatives 
against objectives, constraints, obligations, risk, opportunity, uncertainty, reversibility, 
and information value.
Figure 24. Human Authority Gate. The approving human acts on an exact information and effect boundary.
Layer implementation contract
CONTRACT ENGINEERING DEFINITION
Layer authority Assemble complete, explainable choice packages and compare viable alternatives against 
objectives, constraints, obligations, risk, opportunity, uncertainty, reversibility, and information value.
Boundary
Owns decision definitions, option context, evidence assembly, criteria, trade-offs, recommendation, 
dissent, approval routing, decision records, commitments, and monitoring contracts. A named 
human authority makes the final decision.
Authoritative state Decision definitions and owners; Option and criteria versions; Decision package revisions; Approval, 
dissent, and override state; Final decisions, commitments, and monitoring contracts.
Primary inputs Objectives and constraints; Evidence, assessments, forecasts, scenarios, and simulations; Options 
and stakeholder obligations; Risk appetite, policy, and approval rules.
Canonical outputs Decision Packages; Recommendations and alternatives; Approvals, dissent, overrides, and final 
decision record; Commitments and monitoring contracts; Decision Replay state.
Quality dimensions decision completeness, evidence coverage, alternative coverage, time-to-decision, reversibility, 
approval integrity, monitoring completeness, outcome linkage.

40  Executive Operating System
Govern strategic attention, responsibility, review, commitments, scenarios, decisions, 
material change, and outcomes across institutional cadence.
Figure 25. The Closed Engineering Loop. Each phase commits governed state and evidence for the next.
Layer implementation contract
CONTRACT ENGINEERING DEFINITION
Layer authority Govern strategic attention, responsibility, review, commitments, scenarios, decisions, material 
change, and outcomes across institutional cadence.
Boundary
Owns executive workspaces, attention policy, briefing products, decision rooms, responsibility 
views, strategic cadence, notification, and access modes. It is not a passive dashboard and does 
not create separate truths for different roles.
Authoritative state
Attention and materiality policy; Briefing definitions and versions; Workspace and responsibility 
configuration; Review cadence and agenda state; Notification, suppression, delegation, and 
acknowledgement history.
Primary inputs Material changes and warnings; Objectives, risks, opportunities, and scenarios; Pending decisions 
and commitments; Monitoring, outcomes, and review cadence.
Canonical outputs Executive Briefings; Decision and review rooms; Prioritized attention queues; Commitment and 
outcome reviews; Strategic cadence and institutional state.
Quality dimensions time-to-understanding, decision latency, attention precision, warning acknowledgement, review 
completion, commitment visibility, accessibility, explanation use.

41  Durable Workflow Runtime
Material intelligence work is executed as recoverable state, not as an in-memory chain 
of calls. The workflow runtime preserves intent, authority, progress, evidence, 
deadlines, compensation, and accountable completion across process, host, network, 
and deployment interruption.
Figure 26. Durable Workflow State. Every consequential transition persists before work advances.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Workflow definition Versioned states, transitions, guards, roles, timers, retries, compensations, evidence, and terminal 
conditions.
Workflow instance Durable execution bound to tenant, purpose, initiating actor, definition version, business object, and 
correlation context.
Task An idempotent unit of machine or human work with bounded inputs, side effects, deadlines, and 
completion evidence.
Terminal result Completed, rejected, cancelled, compensated, expired, or failed-with-escalation; no silent or 
ambiguous completion state.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-41-001
Every consequential multi-step operation SHALL execute through 
a versioned durable workflow and persist each committed 
transition before acknowledging it.

conformance test

reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.

42  Human Gates and Approvals
Human authority is implemented as a first-class runtime control. A human gate presents 
the exact decision context, verifies authority and separation of duties, records the 
accountable act, and resumes execution without allowing a model, agent, administrator, 
or timeout to impersonate consent.
Figure 27. Human Authority Gate. The approving human acts on an exact information and effect boundary.

and downstream effect.

Decision record Authenticated choice, rationale, information boundary, reservations, time, authority, and signature or 
equivalent proof.
Gate outcome Approved, rejected, returned, abstained, expired, revoked, or escalated; each is distinct and policy 
governed.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

44  Agent Tool and Capability Control
Tools are controlled institutional capabilities, not model conveniences. Every read, 
computation, network action, mutation, communication, export, and physical or 
enterprise side effect is admitted through a least-privilege capability grant and an 
observable enforcement point.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Tool contract Typed inputs and outputs, side-effect class, data handling, network destinations, timeout, 
idempotency, compensation, and audit profile.
Capability grant Agent, task, tool, action, object scope, environment, budget, duration, purpose, obligations, and 
revocation.
Enforcement point Gateway or sandbox that authenticates the caller, evaluates policy, constrains execution, and 
records the result.
Side-effect class None, reversible, compensatable, approval-required, irreversible, or prohibited.

Evaluation is a continuous control plane that determines whether a model, agent, 
workflow, source, analytical method, package, and composed product remains fit for a 
declared task and consequence class. Aggregate scores never conceal critical failure 
modes.

contamination status.

and use.

outcome.

47  Proactive Warning Runtime
The warning runtime converts material change into accountable attention. It joins weak 
signals, evidence, affected graph and twin state, forecasts, scenarios, strategic 
objectives, and time-to-act without overstating confidence or flooding operators with 
undifferentiated alerts.

uncertainty, and lifecycle.

superseded.

48  Decision Replay and Governed Learning
Replay reconstructs what was knowable, believed, authorized, selected, committed, and 
later observed without hindsight contamination. Learning converts that evidence into 
change proposals that must be evaluated, approved, released, monitored, and 
reversible.
Figure 32. Decision Replay And Learning. Historical state stays distinct from later outcomes and lessons.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION

versions available at decision time.
Outcome record Observed consequences, indicators, attribution limits, external changes, commitments, and 
accountable assessment.
Lesson proposal Evidence-backed change to memory, ontology, model, agent, workflow, policy, control, product, or 
practice.
Learning release Approved versioned change with evaluation, rollout, monitoring, rollback, affected products, and 
effective time.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-48-001

later knowledge only as a separate comparison.

conformance test

for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.

bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-48-003

conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer,

review

FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR

• Golden decision cases reproduce historical state before and after seeded later corrections.
• Replay diff tests prove later evidence cannot contaminate the original boundary.
• Learning release tests trace proposal through approval, evaluation, deployment, monitoring, and 
rollback.
• Audit queries resolve the exact behavior and authority effective for any historical outcome.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE
Decision Replay and Governed Learning is conformant only when every requirement is 
implemented for the declared scope, all failure states are observable and recoverable, and the 
listed evidence resolves to the exact released artifacts and governing versions.

49  Identity and Authentication
Every human, workload, device, source, agent, model, package, and external 
organization acts through a verifiable identity with bounded assurance. Authentication 
establishes who or what is acting; it never establishes authority by itself.
Figure 33. Zero-Trust Request Path. Identity establishes the actor; policy establishes permitted action.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Principal Stable identity for a human, workload, device, source, agent, model, package, or external 
organization.
Credential Short-lived proof bound to principal, issuer, audience, assurance, key, validity, and revocation state.
Delegation Explicit constrained authority chain from a principal to a workload or agent for a stated purpose and 
duration.
Session Authenticated continuity with device, network, risk, assurance, purpose, and reauthentication 
context.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-49-001
All requests SHALL authenticate a principal or be explicitly 
admitted as a public source observation through a source-
specific trust contract.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-49-002
Human authentication SHALL meet policy-defined assurance for 
the requested consequence, data class, administration plane, 
and deployment boundary.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-49-003
Workload, agent, model, package, and device identities SHALL 
be cryptographically bound to approved runtime or artifact state 
and rotated automatically.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-49-004
Delegation SHALL carry actor, delegate, purpose, actions, object 
scope, audience, duration, constraints, and revocation and 
SHALL remain visible downstream.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-49-005
Authentication failure, uncertainty, revocation, or assurance 
downgrade SHALL fail closed for protected actions and invalidate 
affected active sessions according to policy.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Identity provider unavailable Use only pre-authorized bounded continuity for existing low-risk sessions; deny new 
privileged authentication.
Credential suspected 
compromised
Revoke, terminate affected sessions, rotate dependent credentials, and open incident 
impact analysis.

50  Authorization and Policy Enforcement
Authorization evaluates whether a principal may perform an action on an object for a 
purpose in a particular environment and time. Decisions and obligations are enforced at 
every material read, transformation, model, agent, workflow, export, administration, and 
side-effect boundary.
Figure 34. Policy Enforcement Surface. The same decision and obligation semantics apply across product boundaries.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Policy input Principal, delegation, action, object, purpose, classification, relationship, environment, time, location, 
risk, model, agent, and effect.
Decision Allow, deny, indeterminate, or allow-with-obligations for one normalized request and policy version.
Obligation Mask, filter, minimize, log, watermark, localize, approve, expire, encrypt, retain, notify, or prohibit 
onward use.
Enforcement proof Decision ID, input digest, policy version, enforcement point, effect, and resulting object or error.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-50-001
Every protected action SHALL obtain and enforce a policy 
decision at the owning boundary; cached decisions SHALL be 
scope- and time-bounded and revocation aware.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-50-002
Default behavior SHALL be deny when identity, purpose, 
classification, ownership, policy, or enforcement state is missing 
or indeterminate.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-50-003
Object-level and relationship-level controls SHALL survive graph 
traversal, retrieval, summarization, export, model context 
assembly, and derived product creation.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-50-004
Administrative access SHALL use the same policy system with 
stronger assurance, explicit purpose, just-in-time scope, 
monitoring, and independent audit.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-50-005
Authorization semantics and conformance fixtures SHALL remain 
identical across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise 
deployments.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Policy service unavailable Apply a declared restrictive continuity policy or deny; never assume previous broad 
access.

51  Tenant and Domain Isolation
The Customer Intelligence Domain is the primary logical isolation boundary for 
evidence, knowledge, models, agents, policy, decisions, audit, administration, and 
export. Isolation is enforced through identity, authorization, cryptography, runtime 
placement, state partitioning, network controls, telemetry, and verification.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Domain identity Stable tenant and domain identifiers present in every object, request, event, workflow, key, log, 
metric, trace, and package context.
Isolation cell Bounded compute, state, queue, cache, model, agent, network, and operational context assigned to 
one or more policy-compatible domains.
Shared service Control or utility service that processes domain-scoped data without owning cross-domain strategic 
state.
Cross-domain action Explicit governed exchange with origin, recipient, purpose, package, obligations, approval, expiry, 
and audit.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-51-001
All canonical state and operational records SHALL carry 
authenticated domain context and SHALL reject absent, 
conflicting, or untrusted tenant identity.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-51-002
Domain context SHALL be propagated explicitly; process-local, 
connection-local, or user-interface context alone SHALL NOT 
authorize data access.
Negative 
conformance test
Adversarial fixture proving the 
prohibited path is denied and 
audited.
ES-51-003
Caches, indexes, vectors, queues, temporary files, model 
context, logs, traces, backups, and support artifacts SHALL 
enforce the same isolation as canonical stores.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-51-004
Cross-domain search, graph traversal, learning, benchmarking, 
sharing, or administration SHALL require an explicit product 
contract and policy authority.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-51-005
Isolation controls SHALL be continuously tested in every 
deployment mode and after changes to storage, routing, 
observability, model, agent, and package infrastructure.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Domain context mismatch Reject, quarantine the attempted operation, and initiate security analysis when data 
exposure is possible.
Isolation control unavailable Stop affected processing; do not fall back to an unpartitioned path.
Cross-domain package expired Revoke recipient access and execute retention or deletion obligations with evidence.

52  Cryptography, Keys, and Secrets
Cryptography protects identity, confidentiality, integrity, provenance, software supply, 
and non-repudiation across the full lifecycle. Key authority and custody are 
independently governed from data administration and may remain customer-controlled 
by deployment policy.
Figure 35. Cryptographic Key Hierarchy. Custody, purpose, tenant, domain, environment, and lifecycle remain separable.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Key hierarchy Root, tenant, domain, data, service, signing, transport, backup, and ephemeral keys with declared 
owner and cryptoperiod.
Key custody Provider-managed, customer-managed, customer-held, or external authority with explicit operations 
and continuity obligations.
Secret Credential or sensitive value issued at runtime to an authenticated workload for a narrow audience 
and duration.
Crypto evidence Algorithm suite, key reference, operation, time, principal, object, result, rotation, revocation, and 
verification status.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-52-001
Data SHALL be encrypted in transit and at rest using approved, 
versioned cryptographic profiles appropriate to classification and 
deployment policy.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-52-002
Keys SHALL be separated by tenant, domain, purpose, 
environment, and lifecycle where consequence requires; key 
identifiers SHALL remain resolvable historically without exposing 
key material.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-52-003
Secret material SHALL be issued dynamically, memory bounded, 
redacted from logs and artifacts, rotated automatically, and 
prohibited from source or build outputs.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-52-004
Signing keys for software, models, agents, policies, manifests, 
evidence, and exports SHALL be custody-separated from the 
identities that create those artifacts.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-52-005
Rotation, revocation, compromise, cryptographic migration, 
backup recovery, customer exit, and destruction SHALL be 
executable workflows with verified residual-state closure.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
