53  Network Trust and Controlled Egress
Network location is not authority. Every connection is authenticated, authorized, 
encrypted, destination-bound, observable, and constrained by data, purpose, 
deployment, and sovereignty policy. Egress is a governed capability with content-aware 
controls and explicit failure behavior.
Figure 36. Controlled Egress. Every transfer binds destination, purpose, content, policy, and receipt.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Trust zone Workload and data boundary with declared ingress, egress, identity, inspection, administration, and 
failure policy.
Service channel Mutually authenticated, encrypted, authorized communication between registered workload 
identities.
Egress grant Destination, protocol, data class, purpose, principal, workload, duration, volume, inspection, and 
approval.
Transfer record Origin, destination, object or digest, policy, classification, bytes, time, result, and receipt where 
applicable.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-53-001
All service communication SHALL authenticate both endpoints 
and evaluate workload, action, destination, tenant, and purpose 
policy.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-53-002
Production workloads SHALL have no general outbound internet 
access; destinations and protocols SHALL be allow-listed 
through controlled egress points.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-53-003
Egress SHALL enforce classification, rights, minimization, 
residency, purpose, recipient, encryption, rate, inspection, and 
approval obligations before transfer.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-53-004
Disconnected and on-premise modes SHALL use signed import 
and export packages with equivalent policy, provenance, 
malware, integrity, and receipt checks.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-53-005
Network policy, service identity, certificate, destination, and 
egress-decision changes SHALL be versioned, tested, staged, 
monitored, and reversible.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Destination cannot be verified Deny transfer and preserve the requested intent for accountable review.

54  Privacy, Minimization, and Sovereignty
Privacy and sovereignty are executable policy dimensions spanning acquisition, identity, 
data, keys, models, agents, administration, telemetry, support, continuity, export, 
retention, deletion, and exit. They are not location labels or contractual footnotes.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Purpose record Authorized institutional purpose, legal or contractual basis, scope, population, data classes, expiry, 
and owner.
Minimization plan Fields, resolution, history, frequency, identifiers, recipients, retention, model use, and derived 
products strictly required.
Sovereignty profile Independent constraints for data, keys, models, administration, telemetry, supply, support, backup, 
continuity, and exit.
Subject action Access, correction, restriction, objection, portability, deletion, notification, or other applicable 
governed request.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-54-001
Collection and processing SHALL be admitted only for a declared 
purpose with approved authority, scope, minimization, retention, 
and downstream use.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-54-002
Sensitive identifiers SHALL be tokenized, pseudonymized, 
aggregated, masked, or excluded whenever the product contract 
does not require direct identity.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-54-003
Sovereignty policy SHALL be evaluated independently for data 
storage, processing, keys, models, administration, telemetry, 
support, supply, backup, and disaster recovery.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-54-004
Derived objects, embeddings, model context, evaluation data, 
logs, and exports SHALL inherit applicable privacy and

Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-54-005
Subject requests, holds, legal conflicts, deletion, and customer 
exit SHALL use durable workflows with scope proof, residual 
inventory, completion evidence, and independent verification.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Purpose absent or expired Stop new processing and route existing state through retention, restriction, or deletion 
policy.
Sovereignty constraints conflict Deny the operation and require accountable legal, customer, and governance resolution.
Deletion residual remains Keep the request open and expose residual location, reason, expiry, and accountable 
owner.

55  Audit and Non-Repudiation
Audit is an independent, append-only account of consequential system and human 
action. It records who or what acted, under which authority and information boundary, 
what changed or was attempted, what controls decided, and what evidence proves the 
result.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Audit event Actor, delegation, action, target, before/after reference, purpose, policy, result, time, location, trace, 
and evidence digest.
Audit stream Ordered, integrity-protected domain and control-plane record with independent custody and 
retention.
Proof Signature, digest chain, trusted timestamp, receipt, immutable storage evidence, or equivalent 
control appropriate to consequence.
Audit view Policy-filtered query or export that preserves evidentiary meaning without exposing unrelated 
protected content.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-55-001
Consequential reads, writes, policy decisions, model and agent 
runs, human gates, administration, exports, corrections, releases, 
and security events SHALL emit audit records.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-55-002
Audit acceptance SHALL be durable before a consequential 
action is acknowledged complete, or the action SHALL enter an 
explicit unresolved evidence state.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-55-003
Audit records SHALL be append-only, integrity protected, time 
quality marked, access controlled, retained independently, and 
continuously reconciled against source systems.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-55-004
Audit content SHALL identify exact artifact, policy, schema, 
model, agent, workflow, object, and deployment versions material 
to the action.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-55-005
Audit search and export SHALL preserve chain of custody, query 
authority, redaction, completeness scope, and verification 
instructions.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Audit sink unavailable Block or buffer according to consequence policy; never claim verified completion without 
required evidence.
Integrity gap detected Seal affected range, preserve competing copies, alert security and governance, and 
prohibit unsupported non-repudiation claims.
Clock quality degraded Record uncertainty and ordering evidence; do not fabricate precise event time.

56  Software Supply Chain and Marketplaces
Every executable, model, agent, workflow, connector, ontology extension, scenario, 
simulation, policy, and marketplace package is a governed supply-chain artifact. 
Admission depends on declared composition, provenance, evaluation, capabilities, 
compatibility, ownership, monitoring, and revocation.
Figure 37. Supply-Chain Package Promotion. One signed artifact advances through progressively stronger evidence gates.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Package manifest Identity, version, type, publisher, owner, dependencies, interfaces, permissions, data, network, 
models, resources, and compatibility.
Provenance Source, builder, environment, inputs, tests, signatures, attestations, software or model bill of 
materials, and custody.
Admission decision Approved scope, tenant or domain, deployment modes, capabilities, constraints, monitoring, expiry, 
and rollback.
Marketplace state Draft, submitted, quarantined, evaluated, approved, restricted, published, deprecated, suspended, 
revoked, or retired.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-56-001
Production SHALL execute only signed artifacts whose 
provenance, composition, evaluation, vulnerabilities, licenses, 
policy, ownership, and compatibility pass current admission 
policy.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-56-002
Marketplace packages SHALL declare all data access, models, 
tools, network destinations, side effects, secrets, compute, 
storage, telemetry, and human gates before evaluation.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-56-003
Packages SHALL execute in capability-scoped isolation and 
SHALL NOT acquire ambient tenant, network, filesystem, 
credential, administration, or model access.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-56-004 Promotion SHALL preserve artifact identity across environments; 
rebuilds require a new digest and complete evidence chain. Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-56-005
Revocation SHALL stop new use, identify active executions and 
derived products, apply rollback or review policy, and preserve 
historical replayability.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Signature or provenance invalid Quarantine and prohibit execution or import.

57  Quality Contracts and SLOs
Quality is part of product state. Every service and intelligence product declares what 
good means, how it is measured, which population and time window apply, what 
happens when evidence is weak, and which decisions remain safe when an objective is 
missed.
Figure 38. Product Quality Contract. Operational health and intelligence fitness remain distinct but connected.

indicator.
Product contract Completeness, freshness, accuracy, calibration, provenance, coverage, latency, availability, and 
decision-use constraints.
Breach state At risk, breached, exhausted, waived, recovering, or closed with impact, owner, actions, and 
verification.

58  Performance and Capacity Engineering
Performance is engineered against strategic workloads, consequence, and time-to-
decision rather than isolated endpoint speed. Capacity models cover ingestion bursts, 
graph growth, temporal history, twin and simulation portfolios, agent concurrency, model 
contention, human queues, and recovery demand.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Workload class Interactive query, command, stream, batch, workflow, agent, model, graph, simulation, export, 
rebuild, or recovery.
Service envelope Population, concurrency, data shape, latency distribution, throughput, freshness, resource, and 
deadline targets.
Capacity model Demand drivers, unit costs, scaling key, headroom, saturation points, dependencies, and recovery 
reserve.
Admission control Policy that accepts, queues, sheds, degrades, reschedules, or rejects work before unsafe 
saturation.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-58-001
Each component SHALL define workload classes, scaling keys, 
service envelopes, saturation signals, admission behavior, and 
resource budgets.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.

Capacity planning SHALL model normal, forecast, campaign, 
source burst, geopolitical event, warning storm, model outage, 
recovery, and tenant-noise conditions.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-58-003
Interactive and consequential workloads SHALL be protected 
from analytical, training, simulation, export, and rebuild workloads

Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-58-004

Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration,

sovereignty, key locality, state movement, warm-up, model 
availability, and downstream capacity constraints.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Capacity forecast exceeded Apply admission, priority, degradation, and expansion policy before uncontrolled queue 
growth.
Hot tenant or entity Isolate the contention domain and preserve fairness and critical workload reserves.
Dependency saturation Reduce or defer upstream work using propagated backpressure and visible product 
freshness state.

59  Availability and Resilience
Availability means the right capability remains trustworthy for its declared consequence. 
Resilience prevents a local dependency, tenant, source, model, region, administrator, or 
package failure from becoming an uncontrolled system-wide or cross-customer failure.
Figure 39. Degraded Service State Machine. Capability reduces explicitly while trust claims remain intact.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Failure domain Process, host, zone, region, cell, tenant, store, queue, source, model, identity, key, control plane, or 
operator boundary.
Resilience pattern Replication, partition, checkpoint, retry, circuit break, bulkhead, fallback, queue, reconstruction, or 
manual continuity.
Dependency policy Criticality, timeout, retry, fallback, freshness, isolation, recovery, and customer-visible consequence.
Continuity state Normal, constrained, read-only, stale-visible, queued, isolated, unavailable, recovering, or 
reconciled.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

dependencies, required availability, recovery objectives, isolation 
controls, and declared degraded states.

reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.

and subordinate to circuit breaking and system-wide load 
protection.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-59-003

while shared control services SHALL degrade without creating

Fault trace, recovered state digest,

customer-visible mode.
ES-59-004

customer-visible mode.

Regional or cell failure Fail over or isolate under placement policy, expose continuity state, and preserve 
sovereignty constraints.
Control-plane partition Use only bounded pre-authorized continuity; deny state or authority changes that require 
current control.

60  Degraded Service Semantics
Degradation is a named product state with precise guarantees and prohibitions. The 
Eye may reduce capability, currency, automation, or throughput, but it may never 
fabricate freshness, confidence, provenance, authorization, approval, or completeness.
Figure 40. Degraded Service State Machine. Capability reduces explicitly while trust claims remain intact.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Mode Normal, constrained, delayed, stale-visible, read-only, queued, manually supervised, unavailable, or 
recovering.
Entry condition Measured dependency, quality, security, policy, capacity, data, model, or control failure that selects 
the mode.
Guarantee Capabilities and object properties that remain trustworthy in the mode.
Prohibition Actions and claims that are disabled until recovery and reconciliation complete.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-60-001

publish degraded modes, entry and exit criteria, guarantees, 
prohibitions, operator actions, and consumer signals.

reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.

computation time, cause, affected scope, expected review, and 
decision-use constraint.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-60-003
Read-only mode SHALL prevent all indirect writes, side effects, 
acknowledgements, learning releases, approvals, and hidden 
queueing not explicitly declared.

independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-60-004

conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer,

Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Mode cannot be determined Select unavailable or the most restrictive safe mode for the affected consequence.
Consumer ignores degraded state Enforce at the owning boundary where use would be unsafe and record the attempted 
action.
Recovery incomplete Remain recovering and expose unresolved state; do not declare normal from infrastructure 
health alone.

61  Backup, Recovery, and Disaster Recovery
Recovery restores an internally consistent intelligence system, not merely files or 
databases. The plan preserves identity, policy, keys, canonical history, graph and twin 
state, workflows, audit, model and agent artifacts, projections, exports, and customer 
sovereignty within declared loss and time objectives.
Figure 41. Consistent Recovery Chain. Recovery restores the operating system's semantic boundary, not isolated files.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Recovery unit Customer domain, cell, service, canonical store, artifact registry, control plane, region, or full 
deployment.
Recovery point Latest mutually consistent state across authoritative data, events, workflows, audit, policies, keys, 
and manifests.
Recovery time Time from declared incident to verified capability state, including reconciliation and product 
validation.
Recovery evidence Backup identity, integrity, custody, key, restore log, reconciliation, validation, exceptions, and 
approval.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

consistency boundary, retention, encryption, custody, restoration 
order, RPO, RTO, and verification owner.

bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.

artifact, workflow, event, and dependency versions required for 
restoration.

Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-61-003

review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.

63  Incident, Change, and Problem Management
Operational governance treats incidents, changes, and recurring problems as 
connected evidence. Response protects people, customer authority, strategic truth,

restoration speed.

package, or operating procedure.
Problem Underlying or systemic cause whose removal reduces recurrence or consequence across incidents 
and near misses.

obligations, recovery, and learning.

Operational data has the same governed lifecycle discipline as strategic data. Queues, 
caches, indexes, vectors, temporary files, telemetry, support bundles, checkpoints,

authority, retention, reconstruction, and deletion behavior.

proof.
Rebuild contract Canonical inputs, algorithm, version, ordering, checkpoint, reconciliation, target time, and validation.
Residual inventory Known remaining copies after retention, deletion, customer exit, incident, or migration action.

schema, and environment.

requirements.

bound to artifact, configuration,

bound to artifact, configuration,

Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Orphaned state discovered Quarantine, identify ownership and policy, assess exposure, and disposition through an 
audited workflow.
Projection cannot rebuild Treat hidden state as authoritative drift, stop unsupported use, and repair the state 
contract.
Residual copy cannot be deleted Record location, reason, access restriction, expiry, and accountable exception.

65  Repository and Module Standards
Source organization makes ownership, boundaries, contracts, controls, and verification 
visible. Repositories and modules may follow team and deployment needs, but no 
layout may conceal canonical responsibility, shared mutable state, ungoverned 
dependencies, or unauditable generation.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Repository Version-controlled source and declarative assets with owner, purpose, classification, build, tests, 
release, and archival policy.
Module Cohesive implementation unit with explicit public contract, dependencies, state access, 
configuration, telemetry, and tests.
Generated asset Reproducible output with generator, inputs, version, review, digest, and update procedure.
Ownership file Machine-readable mapping from paths and artifacts to accountable engineering, product, security, 
data, and operational owners.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-65-001

dependencies, interfaces, state access, controls, tests, and 
release path.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-65-002

SHALL NOT bypass owners through internal imports, shared 
schemas, direct database access, or generated client 
modifications.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-65-003
Dependency direction SHALL follow declared architecture 
boundaries and SHALL be continuously checked for cycles, 
forbidden imports, and undeclared runtime links.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-65-004
Generated code, schemas, clients, policies, infrastructure, 
prompts, and documentation SHALL be reproducible and 
reviewed from authoritative sources.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-65-005
Secrets, customer content, production identifiers, unlicensed 
data, mutable binaries, and environment-specific authority 
SHALL be prohibited from source repositories.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Owner unavailable Stop material release and invoke service ownership continuity.
Forbidden dependency introduced Fail integration and require boundary correction or approved architecture change.
Generated output differs Reject the artifact until generator, inputs, and reproducibility are reconciled.

66  Configuration and Feature Control
Configuration is versioned product behavior under the same authority as code. Flags, 
policies, schemas, thresholds, routing, models, agents, connectors, workflow definitions, 
and operational limits are typed, owned, validated, staged, observable, and historically 
resolvable.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION

rollback.

experiment.
Feature state Disabled, shadow, internal, restricted, staged, generally enabled, suspended, deprecated, or 
removed.

67  Test Architecture
Testing is a layered proof system. It validates local logic, contracts, canonical 
semantics, policy, security, data quality, AI fitness, resilience, recovery, deployment 
parity, human authority, and institutional outcomes using versioned evidence that can 
be reproduced independently.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Test level Unit, property, component, contract, integration, system, end-to-end, operational, recovery, or 
acceptance.
Conformance fixture Portable canonical input, environment assumptions, expected outputs and effects, tolerances, and 
evidence.
Test population Curated, generated, historical, adversarial, synthetic, or production-shadow data with rights and 
contamination controls.
Evidence record Artifact, environment, dependencies, seed, population, runner, result, logs, exceptions, reviewer, 
and signature.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-67-001 Every normative requirement SHALL map to one or more owned 
verification methods and release evidence records.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-67-002
Contract tests SHALL run for every provider and consumer 
version and include errors, abstention, retries, ordering, 
compatibility, policy, and degraded behavior.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-67-003
Canonical conformance suites SHALL validate identity, time, 
truth, provenance, correction, human authority, deployment 
parity, and replay across the complete strategic loop.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-67-004
AI and probabilistic tests SHALL use distributions, calibration, 
slices, uncertainty, abstention, drift, challenge, and outcome 
measures rather than deterministic exact-match alone.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-67-005
Production-like system tests SHALL include real control 
enforcement, encryption, audit, data scale, temporal depth, graph 
density, model and agent boundaries, and failure injection.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Flaky test Quarantine only with owner, impact, expiry, monitoring, and equivalent release protection; 
never normalize nondeterminism.
Unrepresentative fixture Invalidate unsupported conclusions and rebuild the population with provenance and 
coverage evidence.
Test environment drift Stop promotion until environment identity and production equivalence are restored or 
explicitly bounded.

expiry.

monitoring, rollback, and owners.

69  Environment Promotion
Environments progressively increase consequence without changing product meaning. 
Development, verification, integration, pre-production, production, and recovery 
contexts differ in authority and data, but they resolve one release, contract, policy, and 
deployment-parity baseline.

target environment.
Data boundary Synthetic, masked, generated, approved reference, controlled production copy, or live production 
with purpose and lifecycle.
Drift record Observed divergence from declared environment state with owner, consequence, correction, and 
closure.

70  Deployment Packaging
A deployment package is a complete, portable expression of The Eye for SaaS, Private 
Cloud, or On-Premise operation. It carries application, data, policy, model, agent, 
workflow, infrastructure, migration, observability, operations, verification, and exit 
contracts without creating deployment-specific semantics.

sovereignty boundaries.
Installer Idempotent, resumable, preflighted workflow that realizes and verifies the signed target state.

vulnerability inventory.

71  Operational Acceptance
A capability becomes operational only when product, engineering, security, data, AI, 
privacy, reliability, support, customer, and governance authorities can operate, recover, 
explain, challenge, and retire it. A successful deployment is necessary but insufficient.
Figure 46. Operational Acceptance. Independent authorities close evidence for an exact release and scope.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Acceptance scope Exact release, configuration, tenant or domain, deployment, use cases, consequence classes, 
integrations, and exclusions.
Readiness evidence Conformance, tests, security, privacy, data, AI fitness, SLOs, capacity, recovery, operations, 
support, training, and documentation.
Authority Named accountable roles that accept owned risk and readiness without approving outside their 
domain.
Acceptance state Pending, conditional, accepted, suspended, withdrawn, expired, or retired with effective time and 
obligations.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

review

reservations, and effective time.

fitness, data, operations, and business readiness on behalf of all 
independent authorities.

conformance test

for the exact provider, consumer, 
schema, and environment.
ES-71-003

reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.

72  Engineering Governance and Exceptions
Engineering governance preserves a coherent operating system across teams, 
customers, deployments, and generations of technology. It controls standards, 
requirements, interfaces, object semantics, ownership, exceptions, technical debt, 
deprecation, and evidence without substituting committee opinion for explicit authority.
Figure 47. Engineering Governance Loop. Controlled change preserves architecture and constitutional meaning over time.
Engineering contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Governed artifact Requirement, contract, schema, ontology, policy, model, agent, workflow, package, configuration, 
SLO, test, runbook, or decision record.
Change proposal Current and proposed state, authority, rationale, alternatives, impact, migration, evidence, rollout, 
rollback, and communication.
Exception Narrow time-bounded deviation with owner, scope, risk acceptance, compensating control, 
telemetry, expiry, and closure.
Deprecation Announced lifecycle with replacement, compatibility, migration, support, consumer 
acknowledgement, and final removal evidence.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
ES-72-001
Every governed artifact SHALL have an accountable owner, 
approving authority, lifecycle, version, effective time, 
dependencies, review cadence, and supersession path.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-72-002
Changes to constitutional meaning or frozen architecture SHALL 
follow their controlling amendment authority; Volume 4 
governance cannot approve semantic redesign by exception.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
ES-72-003
Interface, object, policy, model, agent, workflow, and deployment 
changes SHALL include consumer impact, compatibility, 
migration, replay, rollback, and evidence plans before approval.
Fault / recovery test
Fault trace, recovered state digest, 
reconciliation record, and declared 
customer-visible mode.
ES-72-004
Exceptions SHALL NOT remove human final authority, 
provenance, tenant isolation, audit, deployment semantic parity, 
or any constitutional invariant.
Security test
Signed policy/security suite result 
bound to artifact, configuration, 
deployment, and data class.
ES-72-005
Technical debt and deprecation SHALL be maintained as 
decision-active risk with affected objectives, customers, 
consequences, owner, due date, investment, and verified 
closure.
Test + accountable 
review
Executable result plus named 
independent authority, scope, 
reservations, and effective time.
=== PAGE 166 ===
THE EYE VOLUME 4  /  ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED ENGINEERING BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 166
Failure semantics
FAILURE CONDITION REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Authority disputed Preserve the current controlled state and escalate through the specification hierarchy.
Exception expired or expanded Mark non-conformant, block new promotion or use, and execute closure or amendment.
Consumer cannot migrate Maintain declared compatibility or constrain retirement through an approved bounded plan; 
never silently break the contract.
Acceptance evidence
• Governance registry reconciles owners, versions, dependencies, reviews, exceptions, debt, 
deprecations, and closure evidence.
• Change-impact tests trace affected components, contracts, objects, policies, models, agents, 
workflows, deployments, customers, and documents.
• Exception audits verify scope, compensating controls, telemetry, expiry, non-expansion, and 
independent approval.
• Annual engineering-baseline review proves every constitutional invariant and architecture decision 
remains implemented and testable.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE
Engineering Governance and Exceptions is conformant only when every requirement is 
implemented for the declared scope, all failure states are observable and recoverable, and the 
listed evidence resolves to the exact released artifacts and governing versions.
CONSTITUTIONAL INHERITANCE  /  C-004  ·  C-008  ·  C-035  ·  C-042  ·  C-049  ·  C-051  ·  C-052

Common interface obligations
• Canonical envelope, version negotiation, normalized error model, idempotency or ordering 
boundary, deadline, policy, telemetry, and audit causation.
• No direct cross-owner database write, unversioned payload, hidden retry, silent partial result, or 
deployment-specific semantic path.
• Provider and consumer conformance fixtures cover normal, unauthorized, invalid, conflicting, 
abstained, stale, degraded, replay, and recovery behavior.

C  Canonical Object Schema Profiles
Each canonical intelligence object inherits the universal header. This appendix defines 
the minimum domain payload and lifecycle profile; domain schemas may add governed 
fields without removing universal identity, time, truth, provenance, policy, quality, and 
correction obligations.

D  Canonical Envelope Field Dictionary
Envelope fields carry the identity, intent, semantics, time, trust, control, operations, and 
integrity required to preserve meaning across APIs, events, workflows, agents, tools, 
models, deployments, and external transfer.
FIELD FAMILY DEFINITION PRESENCE APPLIES TO
message_id Identity Globally unique immutable message identifier. Required All
tenant_id Identity Authenticated customer tenant boundary. Required All
domain_id Identity Customer Intelligence Domain within the tenant. Required All
principal_id Identity Authenticated human, workload, agent, device, or 
source identity. Required All
delegation_id Identity Reference to constrained delegated authority where 
applicable. Conditional Commands / queries / 
tools
purpose_id Intent Approved institutional purpose for processing. Required All protected 
operations
action Intent Normalized requested action or committed transition. Required All
side_effect_class Intent None, reversible, compensatable, approval-required, 
irreversible, or prohibited. Required Commands / tools
object_type Semantics Canonical object or contract type. Required All
object_id Semantics Stable canonical identity when the operation addresses 
an object. Conditional Object operations
object_version Semantics Exact version read, proposed, or committed. Conditional Versioned objects
schema_version Semantics Payload schema identifier and version. Required All
ontology_version Semantics Semantic ontology version required to interpret content. Conditional Semantic products
truth_state Semantics Observed, claimed, inferred, assessed, decided, 
synthetic, withdrawn, or superseded. Conditional Intelligence objects
event_time Time When the represented event occurred in the world. Conditional Event-bearing objects
observation_time Time When a source was observed. Conditional Observations / 
evidence
valid_time Time World-time interval for which the assertion applies. Conditional Temporal objects
system_time Time When The Eye admitted or changed the version. Required Committed products
issued_at Time Envelope creation time with clock quality. Required All
deadline Time Latest useful or permitted completion time. Conditional Commands / queries / 
tasks
expires_at Time Time after which the envelope, grant, or result is invalid. Conditional Temporary authority / 
results
source_refs Trust References to source and Evidence Objects. Conditional Derived intelligence
lineage_ref Trust Provenance graph root for the product or transition. Conditional Derived / committed 
products
confidence Trust Calibrated confidence representation with method. Conditional Inferred / assessed 
products
quality_state Trust Decision-active quality, freshness, coverage, and 
completeness state. Required Products
classification Control Sensitivity and handling classification. Required All protected content
policy_version Control Policy set used for admission or effect. Required All protected 
operations
obligations Control Machine-enforceable duties returned by policy. Conditional Allow-with-obligations
residency_profile Control Placement and processing sovereignty constraints. Conditional Governed data
retention_profile Control Retention, hold, archive, correction, and deletion policy. Conditional Persistent content
correlation_id Operations End-to-end business or workflow correlation identifier. Required All

E  Standard Error Code Catalog
Errors are typed product states. They never disclose unauthorized existence, collapse 
abstention into failure, imply success for an unknown external effect, or invite unsafe 
retry.
CODE MACHINE NAME CONDITION RETRY CALLER ACTION
EYE-REQ-001 invalid_request Request cannot be interpreted under the 
declared contract. No Correct fields or schema; retain 
correlation.
EYE-REQ-002 unsupported_version Contract, schema, ontology, model, or 
package version is unsupported. No Negotiate a supported version or 
migrate.
EYE-IDN-001 authentication_required No acceptable principal proof is present. No Authenticate at required assurance.
EYE-IDN-002 authentication_failed Credential, audience, issuer, device, or 
assurance validation failed. No Reauthenticate; investigate 
repeated failure.
EYE-AUT-001 access_denied Current policy denies the normalized action. No Do not retry unchanged; request 
authorized review.
EYE-AUT-002 policy_indeterminate Authorization cannot be safely resolved. Bounded Retry only under published 
continuity policy.
EYE-AUT-003 obligation_unmet A required control obligation cannot be 
enforced. No Satisfy the obligation or change the 
request.
EYE-TEN-001 domain_context_invalid Tenant or domain context is absent, 
conflicting, or untrusted. No Correct authenticated routing 
context.
EYE-STA-001 object_not_found No authorized object version matches the 
request. No Verify identity and temporal scope.
EYE-STA-002 version_conflict Expected and current authoritative versions 
differ. No Read current state and resubmit 
intentionally.
EYE-STA-003 truth_state_prohibited The operation is not valid for the object's 
truth state. No Use the permitted adjudication or 
correction path.
EYE-TMP-001 temporal_scope_invalid Time interval, clock quality, or as-of 
boundary is invalid. No Correct explicit temporal 
parameters.
EYE-PRV-001 provenance_incomplete Required source or transformation lineage is 
missing. No Repair lineage or keep product 
quarantined.
EYE-QUA-001 quality_insufficient Product quality is below declared decision-
use threshold. Bounded Use declared degraded path or 
await refresh.
EYE-QUA-002 result_abstained The method cannot support a responsible 
result. No Provide more evidence or route to 
human analysis.
EYE-WFL-001 workflow_conflict Transition is invalid for current durable state. No Refresh instance and use a 
permitted command.
EYE-WFL-002 human_gate_required A named human authorization is required 
before continuation. No Complete the bound human gate.
EYE-WFL-003 deadline_exceeded Work cannot complete within its useful or 
authorized deadline. No Create a new request with reviewed 
context.
EYE-AGT-001 capability_denied Agent lacks a valid grant for the tool, data, or 
effect. No Supervisor may issue a bounded 
grant after review.
EYE-MDL-001 no_conforming_model No approved model satisfies task and policy 
constraints. Bounded Await approved capacity or change 
permitted scope.
EYE-DEP-001 dependency_unavailabl
e
Required dependency is unavailable within 
the operation deadline. Yes Retry with server backoff and same 
idempotency key.
EYE-CAP-001 capacity_exhausted Admission control cannot safely accept 
current work. Yes Retry after declared interval or 
reduce scope.
EYE-DEG-001 capability_degraded Requested capability is constrained by an 
active degraded mode. Bounded Observe guarantees, prohibitions, 
and retry guidance.
EYE-INT-001 integrity_failure Digest, signature, ordering, or custody 
verification failed. No Quarantine and investigate; never 
auto-repair evidence.
EYE-AUD-001 audit_unavailable Required durable evidence cannot be 
committed. Bounded Follow consequence-specific 
continuity; do not claim completion.
EYE-EXT-001 external_effect_unknow
n
Dispatch occurred but external completion 
cannot be proven. No Reconcile by receipt before retry or 
compensation.

THE EYE VOLUME 4  /  ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED ENGINEERING BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 182
CODE MACHINE NAME CONDITION RETRY CALLER ACTION
EYE-RCV-001 reconciliation_required Recovery or failover left state requiring 
deterministic reconciliation. No Complete reconciliation before 
normal writes.
EYE-GOV-001 conformance_blocked Artifact or operation lacks current governing 
evidence. No Close evidence or obtain an 
authorized exception.
Error envelope obligations
• Error code, correlation, causation, trace, request contract version, policy-safe human message, 
retry classification, deadline, degraded state, and support reference.
• No stack trace, secret, model prompt, protected identifier, unauthorized object existence, cross-
tenant signal, or internal topology in caller-visible content.
• Unknown external effects return reconciliation-required state; clients must not retry until the effect 
boundary is resolved.

F  Quality and SLO Catalog
These baseline objectives establish the minimum quality-control vocabulary. 
Component owners refine populations and targets by consequence and deployment 
profile without weakening constitutional safety or product transparency.
ID INDICATOR MEASURED CONDITION BASELINE 
OBJECTIVE
WINDO
W BREACH BEHAVIOR

evidence
SLO-019 Tenant isolation No unauthorized cross-domain disclosure 
or effect 100% Continuous Stop affected plane and 
incident
SLO-020 Backup restore 
verification
Scheduled backups restore and reconcile 
successfully 100% sampled Monthly Escalate recovery risk
SLO-021 Telemetry correlation Consequential flows preserve trace and 
causation 99.99% 24 hours Mark evidence incomplete
SLO-022 Deployment parity Canonical conformance suite equivalent 
across profiles 100% Per release Block affected deployment
SLO RULE
An error budget controls change velocity; it does not authorize loss of human decision sovereignty, 
provenance, tenant isolation, policy enforcement, audit, temporal truth, or deployment semantic 
parity.

G  Test and Conformance Matrix
The test architecture supplies independent evidence from local logic through operational 
acceptance. High-consequence fixtures remain immutable, portable, rights-controlled, 
and reproducible across supported deployment modes.

EVIDENCE 
DIMENSION REQUIRED CONTENT
Identity Requirement, test, fixture, artifact, configuration, schema, policy, model, agent, workflow, 
environment, and deployment versions.
Execution Runner, identity, time, seed, inputs, dependencies, commands, result, logs, traces, resource, and 
exception context.
Validity Population, coverage, tolerances, exclusions, known limitations, contamination state, expiry, and 
reviewer.
Integrity Digests, signatures, attestations, custody, immutable storage, and independent reproduction 
instructions.

H  Configuration Namespace
Configuration names encode authority and scope. Every item is typed, versioned, 
signed, reviewed, promoted, observable, historically resolvable, and removable through 
the same controlled delivery system as code.
NAMESPACE CONTENT OWNING AUTHORITY PERMITTED 
SCOPES
eye.identity.* Identity assurance, issuers, sessions, workload 
identity Security identity authority Deployment / tenant
eye.policy.* Authorization policy bundles, precedence, 
obligations, cache bounds Policy authority Global / deployment / 
tenant
eye.domain.* Customer Intelligence Domain placement and 
isolation Platform authority Tenant / domain
eye.crypto.* Cryptographic profiles, key references, rotation and 
custody Cryptographic authority Deployment / tenant / 
domain
eye.network.* Trust zones, service channels, ingress and egress 
grants Network security Deployment / cell / 
workload
eye.observation.* Source contracts, schedules, quotas, correction 
behavior Observation authority Domain / source
eye.objects.* Object schemas, admission rules, lifecycle and 
projections Data contract authority Global / domain
eye.graph.* Ontology, revision, inference, traversal and 
projection limits Knowledge authority Global / domain
eye.twins.* Twin classes, mappings, reconciliation and branch 
policy Twin authority Domain / twin class
eye.models.* Model profiles, routing, fallback, context and 
retention AI platform authority Deployment / domain / 
task
eye.agents.* Agent admission, capabilities, budgets, tools and 
supervision Agent platform authority Domain / agent / task
eye.workflows.* Definitions, timers, retries, gates, compensation 
and migration Workflow authority Global / domain / 
definition
eye.quality.* Indicators, SLOs, thresholds, freshness and 
product constraints Quality authority Component / product
eye.features.* Controlled feature lifecycle and eligible scope Product and release authority Deployment / tenant / 
role
eye.capacity.* Admission, quotas, priorities, resource and cost 
budgets SRE authority Cell / tenant / 
workload
eye.telemetry.* Signal schemas, sampling, redaction, residency 
and retention Observability authority Deployment / tenant / 
component
eye.retention.* Retention, hold, archive, correction, deletion and 
residual policy Data governance Tenant / object class
eye.release.* Candidate, rollout, stop, rollback and acceptance 
controls Release authority Environment / 
deployment
Prohibited configuration patterns
• Unowned free-form keys, secrets, customer content, unbounded global overrides, runtime shell 
mutation, deployment-specific semantic defaults, or settings without schema and expiry.
• Feature flags that bypass authorization, human gates, audit, provenance, tenant isolation, 
evaluation, migration, or operational acceptance.
• Manual production-only values that cannot be reproduced, reviewed, promoted, rolled back, or 
resolved during Decision Replay.

Constitutional implementation matrix
ID INVARIANT BINDING STATEMENT PRIMARY VOLUME 4 
ANCHORS
C-001 Category integrity
The Eye shall be conceived, designed, sold, and operated as a Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System. It shall not be reduced to a chatbot, dashboard, BI 
tool, copilot, or reporting product.
Ch. 1, Ch. 4, Ch. 5
C-002 Closed strategic loop
The product shall implement one continuous operating loop: Observe, 
Understand, Predict, Simulate, Decide, and Learn. No stage may be treated as a 
disconnected feature island.
Ch. 5, Ch. 47
C-003 Product boundary Interfaces such as chat, dashboards, reports, and APIs may exist, but they are 
access modes to the operating system rather than the product definition. Ch. 1, Ch. 4, Ch. 40, Ch. 2
C-004 Human decision 
sovereignty
A named human authority shall make every final strategic or high-impact decision. 
The system may advise, prepare, and execute approved workflows, but it shall not 
silently assume decision rights.
Ch. 2, Ch. 5, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, 
Ch. 43, Ch. 44, Ch. 48, Ch. 49, 
Ch. 50, Ch. 55, Ch. 60, Ch. 63, 
Ch. 66, Ch. 67, Ch. 71, Ch. 72, 
Ch. 39, Ch. 40, Ch. 1
C-005 Explain every 
recommendation
Every material forecast, scenario, simulation result, risk, opportunity, and 
recommendation shall expose evidence, assumptions, uncertainty, lineage, 
alternatives, and accountable system versions.
Ch. 3, Ch. 5, Ch. 6, Ch. 18, 
Ch. 20, Ch. 21, Ch. 26, Ch. 27, 
Ch. 28, Ch. 42, Ch. 43, Ch. 45, 
Ch. 46, Ch. 47, Ch. 48, Ch. 55, 
Ch. 57, Ch. 60, Ch. 62, Ch. 63, 
Ch. 67, Ch. 71, Ch. 32, Ch. 36, 
Ch. 37, Ch. 38, Ch. 39
C-006 Enterprise operating 
standard
The common platform shall satisfy enterprise requirements for governance, 
security, scale, auditability, reliability, and lifecycle management from its first 
production architecture.
Ch. 1, Ch. 2
C-007 Universal core, governed 
specialization
A single domain-neutral core shall serve all customer classes. Domain ontologies, 
models, workflows, agents, and policies may extend the core without forking its 
constitutional semantics.
Ch. 4, Ch. 14, Ch. 34, Ch. 6
C-008 Ten-layer architecture
The ten named architecture layers are the canonical product structure. Cross-
cutting planes may support them but shall not replace, collapse, or rename them 
without constitutional amendment.
Ch. 1, Ch. 4, Ch. 11, Ch. 65, 
Ch. 72, Ch. 31
C-009 Knowledge Graph 
centrality
The Knowledge Graph shall be the connected semantic heart of The Eye and the 
shared context substrate for memory, twins, reasoning, prediction, scenarios, 
simulations, and decisions.
Ch. 25, Ch. 34
C-010 Truth-state separation
Observed evidence, extracted claims, inferred relationships, model outputs, 
human judgments, and synthetic scenario data shall remain explicitly 
distinguishable throughout the system.
Ch. 5, Ch. 12, Ch. 20, Ch. 21, 
Ch. 24, Ch. 25, Ch. 27, Ch. 32, 
Ch. 34, Ch. 35
C-011 Temporal truth
Material facts and relationships shall preserve event time, observation time, 
validity time, and system-record time so that past world states can be 
reconstructed without hindsight contamination.
Ch. 3, Ch. 12, Ch. 17, Ch. 18, 
Ch. 19, Ch. 20, Ch. 22, Ch. 23, 
Ch. 24, Ch. 26, Ch. 30, Ch. 41, 
Ch. 48, Ch. 61, Ch. 64, Ch. 67, 
Ch. 32, Ch. 33, Ch. 34, Ch. 35
C-012 End-to-end provenance
Every material intelligence object shall carry source, chain of custody, 
transformation history, ownership, rights, quality, and version lineage back to its 
evidence.
Ch. 6, Ch. 17, Ch. 19, Ch. 20, 
Ch. 24, Ch. 28, Ch. 30, Ch. 43, 
Ch. 44, Ch. 45, Ch. 55, Ch. 62, 
Ch. 64, Ch. 31, Ch. 32, Ch. 34
C-013 Observation breadth The World Observation Layer shall support the full canonical source universe and 
allow governed custom sources without architectural rework. Ch. 31, Ch. 32
C-014 Freshness and quality 
contracts
Sources, derived data, twins, models, and intelligence products shall declare 
measurable freshness, completeness, quality, and expiry expectations. Ch. 31, Ch. 32
C-015 Durable institutional 
memory
The Eye shall preserve institutional context, decisions, assumptions, evidence, 
outcomes, and lessons as durable memory, subject to lawful retention, deletion, 
and customer policy.
Ch. 12, Ch. 28, Ch. 33, Ch. 34
C-016 Memory governance
Memory shall be permission-aware, tenant-isolated, versioned, retrievable, 
correctable, and protected from unverified model output becoming accepted 
organizational truth.
Ch. 8, Ch. 12, Ch. 18, Ch. 24, 
Ch. 25, Ch. 29, Ch. 30, Ch. 51, 
Ch. 54, Ch. 64, Ch. 33

C-017 Strategy Graph
Objectives, hypotheses, decisions, initiatives, capabilities, resources, indicators, 
risks, opportunities, and outcomes shall be connected in a governed Strategy 
Graph anchored to the Knowledge Graph.
Ch. 34
C-018 Grounded Digital Twins
Every Digital Twin shall be grounded in evidence, graph entities, temporal state, 
declared assumptions, and calibrated models; it shall never present synthetic state 
as observed fact.
Ch. 27, Ch. 35, Ch. 38
C-019 Twin versionability Digital Twins shall support time travel, branching, comparison, replay, 
reconciliation, and versioned evolution across actual and simulated states. Ch. 26, Ch. 35, Ch. 38
C-020 Canonical prediction 
horizons
The Prediction Engine shall support 30-day, 90-day, 180-day, 1-year, 3-year, and 
5-year horizons with horizon-appropriate methods and uncertainty. Ch. 47, Ch. 57, Ch. 36
C-021 Probabilistic forecasting
Forecasts shall communicate distributions, confidence, calibration, drivers, 
sensitivity, and failure conditions rather than false precision or unsupported 
certainty.
Ch. 21, Ch. 27, Ch. 46, Ch. 57, 
Ch. 36
C-022 Scenario plurality
Scenario Intelligence shall maintain multiple coherent futures, including baseline, 
upside, downside, disruption, and user-defined alternatives; no single forecast 
shall masquerade as destiny.
Ch. 27, Ch. 47, Ch. 37
C-023 Reproducible simulation
Every simulation shall declare initial state, assumptions, interventions, model 
versions, constraints, random seeds where applicable, outputs, and sensitivity so 
results can be reproduced and challenged.
Ch. 27, Ch. 35, Ch. 37, Ch. 38
C-024 Decision completeness
Decision Intelligence shall compare viable options against explicit objectives, 
constraints, risks, opportunities, trade-offs, second-order effects, reversibility, and 
information value.
Ch. 21, Ch. 42, Ch. 39
C-025 Decision Replay
The Eye shall preserve the state of knowledge available at decision time and 
support replay against later evidence and outcomes without rewriting the historical 
record.

governed interfaces so customers retain control over cost, sovereignty,

Health Score

property

J  Architecture Decision Implementation Obligations
The accepted Volume 3 decisions below are engineering obligations. Implementation 
detail may evolve behind them, but releases cannot contradict their semantics or treat a 
local exception as architecture change.

THE EYE VOLUME 4  /  ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED ENGINEERING BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 194
K  Engineering Glossary
These definitions are canonical for engineering, assurance, delivery, and operations. 
Domain specifications may add narrower terms but must not redefine the concepts 
below.
TERM ENGINEERING DEFINITION
Acceptance evidence Signed, versioned proof that a defined requirement or readiness condition was evaluated for an exact 
artifact and scope.
Admission control A boundary decision that accepts, queues, sheds, degrades, reschedules, or rejects work before 
unsafe execution.
Artifact identity Content digest plus declared type, version, provenance, and signing context that uniquely binds a 
release input or output.
Authority boundary The point at which one accountable owner may commit state or action and other parties must interact 
by contract.
Capability grant Revocable task-specific authorization for an agent or workload to use a tool, data scope, model, 
network path, resource, or side effect.
Canonical state Authoritative versioned state governed by an owning component and preserved for temporal truth and 
replay.
Compensation Explicit action that addresses the institutional effects of a committed step when atomic rollback is 
impossible.
Conformance fixture Portable canonical input, environment assumptions, expected behavior, tolerances, and evidence 
used across implementations.
Consequence class Declared level of potential institutional, human, financial, legal, security, or strategic effect that 
controls assurance.
Control plane Cross-cutting service and authority for identity, policy, trust, workflow, audit, evaluation, learning, 
deployment, or sovereignty.
Correction propagation Durable identification, invalidation, review, and reconciliation of products affected by corrected or 
withdrawn information.
Customer Intelligence Domain Primary logical isolation boundary for a customer's evidence, knowledge, models, agents, policies, 
decisions, and audit.
Decision-active Permitted to influence an assessment, warning, recommendation, approval, commitment, or 
executive action under current policy and quality.
Degraded mode Named runtime state with explicit guarantees, prohibitions, entry conditions, exit criteria, and 
consumer signals.
Deployment semantic parity Equivalent objects, contracts, workflows, authority, controls, audit, quality, and portability across 
supported deployment modes.
Durable workflow Persisted execution state that survives interruption and preserves transitions, timers, retries, 
compensation, human gates, and evidence.
Effect receipt Verifiable acknowledgement from an external or authoritative boundary that a specific idempotent 
side effect completed.
Error budget Allowed unreliability for a quality objective; it never authorizes breach of a constitutional or security 
control.
Evidence closure State in which all required verification records are current, authentic, scoped correctly, and linked to 
the exact artifact.
Failure domain Boundary within which one defect or outage is expected to remain isolated.
Fitness decision Governed approval, constraint, shadow status, suspension, withdrawal, or indeterminate result for an 
exact artifact and use.
Human gate Durable transition requiring an authenticated named human role to act under verified authority and an 
exact information boundary.
Idempotency boundary Scope within which repeated delivery of the same intent cannot create an additional institutional 
effect.
Immutable promotion Advancement of the same artifact digest across environments without rebuild or manual replacement.
Intelligence Cell Scalable and isolated deployment and failure unit for a bounded set of customer or domain 
workloads.
=== PAGE 195 ===
THE EYE VOLUME 4  /  ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED ENGINEERING BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 195
TERM ENGINEERING DEFINITION
Material change Change significant enough by consequence, urgency, confidence, reversibility, exposure, or 
information value to require accountable attention.
Model gateway Governed boundary that resolves and invokes approved models according to task, data, quality, 
sovereignty, cost, and failure policy.
Non-repudiation Evidence sufficient to attribute a consequential act and verify its integrity, authority, time, and result.
Operational acceptance Governed authorization to operate an exact capability and scope after cross-domain readiness 
evidence closes.
Policy obligation Mandatory handling action returned with an authorization decision, such as masking, logging, 
localization, approval, or expiry.
Projection Rebuildable access representation derived from declared canonical state and algorithms.
Quality contract Measurable agreement defining indicator, population, objective, window, consequence, degraded 
behavior, and evidence.
Reconciliation Deterministic comparison and repair that restores consistency across authoritative state, events, 
workflows, audit, and projections.
Recovery point objective Maximum acceptable authoritative state loss measured against a mutually consistent recovery 
boundary.
Recovery time objective Maximum time to restore and verify a declared product capability, including reconciliation.
Release candidate Immutable complete artifact set evaluated for promotion as one governed unit.
Release gate Policy decision over required evidence, authority, exceptions, scope, and artifact identity before 
promotion.
Residual state Known copy or effect remaining after deletion, correction, revocation, rollback, migration, or customer 
exit.
Side-effect class Classification of an action as none, reversible, compensatable, approval-required, irreversible, or 
prohibited.
Source of truth The explicitly declared owner of authoritative state for one object and lifecycle; it is never inferred 
from convenience or query speed.
State owner Exactly one accountable component authorized to commit the canonical lifecycle of a state class.
System time When The Eye admitted or changed an object version, used to reconstruct what the platform knew at 
a historical instant.
Temporal truth Preservation of event, observation, valid, and system time without overwriting historical knowledge 
state.
Truth state Explicit distinction among observed, claimed, inferred, assessed, decided, synthetic, withdrawn, and 
superseded information.
Verification class Inspection, analysis, automated test, fault injection, security test, operational exercise, or accountable 
review.
Workload identity Cryptographically verifiable identity of a running service, job, agent, model, device, or integration.
Baseline closure
Volume 4 closes when every deployable behavior can identify its controlling constitutional invariant, 
architecture layer and component, canonical object and interface, state owner, control and trust 
boundary, quality contract, failure and recovery semantics, deployment-parity fixture, release evidence, 
operational authority, and retirement path.
END STATE
The Eye is engineered here as one persistent Strategic Intelligence Operating System: 
continuously observing, constructing governed understanding, remembering permanently, 
connecting knowledge, representing the enterprise and world through living twins, forecasting and 
simulating possible futures, preparing explainable recommendations for named human decision-
makers, governing commitments, and learning only through accountable release.
CONSTITUTIONAL INHERITANCE  /  C-001  ·  C-004  ·  C-005  ·  C-035  ·  C-042  ·  C-051  ·  C-052
